# Backplane: whole-codebase review

- Commit `9c6c5448a514ebcddca5a776933793e25179a796` on `main`, level with `origin/main` on 2026-11-16. Scope: every tracked file (3,516), not a diff.
- Method: the tree was mapped (`00-map.md`), cut into 61 review units (`coverage.md`), and each unit was read in full by one reviewer with the callers and callees across its boundary. A cross-cutting pass then re-traced the High findings of the smaller models, resolved the 489 questions the reviewers raised about each other's code, and checked project-level health (`90-cross-cutting.md`). Missing features are in `95-missing-features.md`.
- Models: Fable 5.2 reviewed the 39 high-risk units and did the cross-cutting pass, the missing-features analysis and this report; Opus 5.5 reviewed 10 medium-risk units; Sonnet 5.5 reviewed 7 low-risk units and the 5 samples. Every High finding from an Opus or Sonnet unit was re-traced by the lead before it was kept (`_verify.md`).
- No source file was modified. Nothing was built; the tests that could run on this machine were run (see "Test, lint and audit results").

## Summary

**What it is.** Backplane is an open-source workspace for agentic hardware development. One hub per machine (Rust, `native/host`) starts and supervises coding-agent CLIs, lets them edit KiCad boards and schematics through a KiCad fork's IPC API and FreeCAD by script, and records everything in an append-only event log. A GPUI desktop client and native iOS and Android apps connect to the hub. Machines of one owner link to each other; a guard program restarts and rolls back the hub; a small order server quotes and sells fabricated boards.

**Overall health.** The tree is eight weeks old and has 7,244 commits, almost all written by coding agents and landed through a script that tests before it pushes. It has real strengths: a single-writer append-only log, one audited place where child processes are made, a guard for running on copied data, decision rules written as pure functions with about 4,200 unit tests, a guard program that shares no code with the hub, and source comments that say plainly what is not ported. It also carries the marks of its speed. The port from the removed JavaScript hub is unfinished in many corners (502 feature-gap rows: stubs, ignored options, requests no client sends). The README and 18 docs still describe the old tree. Above all, the failure paths are thin: the test stand-ins for agents cannot fail, so the code that runs when an agent crashes, a disk fills, a peer sleeps, a file is hostile or a restart lands in the middle of a job is mostly untested, and that is where most High findings sit.

No Critical finding was confirmed: the review found no unauthenticated remote code execution and no corruption of the event log in normal operation. **79 High findings stand (69 confirmed, 10 possible with a stated condition), 608 Medium (423 confirmed) and 744 Low**, after merging duplicates. This is a large number, and it should be read against the size of the tree (about 18 MB of first-party code) and its age.

**Top three risks.**

1. **An agent is not contained by the product's own approvals and rules.** Every agent is told where the hub's home is and can read the pairing token there, then call the hub as the person, including answering its own approval cards (`native/host/src/agents/proc.rs:299`). An enforced rule's unlock word is accepted from any "user" message, which an agent can send to itself (`agents/tools.rs:188`). A thread's tool address and key are on the agent's command line, readable by any local user, and give vault values through `backplane/mcp_spec` (`agents/mcp.rs:126`, `mcpx/svc.rs:101`). Unattended mail runs answer permission prompts "allow" unless a short deny-list matches (`bots/mail/charter.rs:16`). An agent can widen an MCP server's scope without the confirm card (`mcpx/tools.rs:169`). A file link in an agent's reply runs a launcher file on one click (`files/misc.rs:75`). Both browsers that agents drive run without Chromium's sandbox (`tools/browser/src/main.ts:248`, `native/gpui/src/viewers/cef.rs:410`).
2. **A person's design work can be lost or overwritten on ordinary paths.** Any hub stop, restart or update kills the embedded KiCad editors without saving (`kicad/embed.rs:871`). While an editor is starting or unresponsive, an agent's write goes to a second, headless KiCad on the same files, and a headless save can overwrite a file that changed outside it (`kicad/embed.rs:794`, `kicad/ipc.rs:564`). Forking a thread overwrites the new worktree with an old checkpoint, and merge clean-up can remove another thread's worktree and branch (`commands/worktree.rs:138`, `commands/merge.rs:68`). Review, BOM, Checks and Files of a hardware thread read the code worktree, which no longer holds the design (`files/rv.rs:375`). A failed commit to the event log is told only to its caller while agents carry on (`hub.rs:369`). A home move is not transactional and can leave the home live on neither machine or on both (`net/homemove.rs:607`).
3. **Input and links from outside are trusted too far.** Design files are read inside the hub with no limits, so a downloaded project can take the hub down in at least six ways (`kicad/watch.rs:545`, `kicad/gerber/rs274x.rs:135`, `kicad/pcb3d/models.rs:160`, `:489`, `kicad/plotread.rs:119`, `md/doc.rs:305`). Machines paired by an `http://` address exchange the vault, hook secrets, the push key and account tokens in clear, and request signatures cover neither route nor direction (`net/pairflow.rs:172`, `net/homemove.rs:587`, `settings/accounts_sync.rs:58`). The guard for running on copied data disarms itself after one guarded run (`main.rs:139`). The release workflow builds, signs and publishes any `v*` tag on any commit, with the signing secrets readable from any pushed branch (`.github/workflows/release.yml:7`, `:424`). The built-in order server is a bare IP address (`settings/fab.rs:13`), and two unauthenticated requests can exhaust the order server's memory (`order/fabsrv/app.py:136`).

**Top three improvements by leverage.**

1. **Draw one boundary around agents.** Give agents a lock-only credential instead of the home path; move the tool key off the command line; record in each message event who wrote it and let rule checks and approvals read only the person's; make unattended runs default-deny. This closes most of risk 1 with changes in four files' worth of code paths.
2. **Move work on untrusted input out of the hub process, under limits.** The reader program `bp-plot` already exists; running board, schematic, Gerber, 3D, embedded-file and README work there with a memory cap, a deadline and a size check turns a dozen findings into one boundary.
3. **Make failure a first-class state, and test it.** A hub-wide "writes failing" state; journals for the multi-step jobs (home move, `kicad_edit`, merge clean-up, owed restarts); an ordered stop for editors that update and restart wait for; and stand-in agents that can fail, so these paths have tests.

**How to read the rest.** The findings below are the High ones in full and the confirmed Medium ones in short form. Every finding of every severity, in full text, is in `findings-all.md`; each unit file `NN-<name>.md` holds its reviewer's summary, improvements, feature gaps, missing tests and questions. The quick wins in "Improvements" are the place to start.

## Findings

Counts after merging duplicates: Critical 0; High 79 (69 confirmed, 10 possible); Medium 608 (423 confirmed, 185 possible); Low 744 (458 confirmed, 286 possible). "Possible" rows state the condition that would trigger them. The Unit column names the unit file with the reviewer's notes; `questions` means the row came from the cross-unit question pass. Locations are relative to the repository root.

### Critical

None.

### High (79)

In full. Rows from Opus and Sonnet units were re-traced by the lead; rows the re-trace lowered are in the Medium table with the reason.

| High | Confirmed | `native/host/src/hub.rs:369` | `Hub::writer`: when `append_many` fails (lines 369-370) the batch's callers get `Err` and the transforms are told, and that is all: no log line, no `guard::degraded`, no frame to clients, no held writes. `guard::degraded("log writes")` is set only at start (hub.rs:136). 97 call sites discard the result (`let _ = hub.commit...`), and the agents' actor only prints and counts (agents/mod.rs:641, agents/tools.rs:16). `docs/store-checkpoints.md` ("Disk full") and `docs/HUB-RESILIENCE.md:56` describe a waiting queue and a degraded state that the Rust writer does not have. | Disk full, quota or EIO while a turn runs: every MessagePosted, ActivityLogged and TurnChanged of the turn is dropped, the agent keeps editing files and running tools, the thread looks frozen, and after space returns the history has a hole and the model still says the turn runs. `state/host.rs:25` also refolds the whole log after each failed batch. | In the writer's error branch: log the error, set `guard::degraded("log writes", ..)` (clear it on the next success), push a notice frame to every client, and expose a `writes_failing()` flag that agents check before starting a turn or a tool that writes. Longer term a bounded wait queue with retry, as the doc describes. | 01 |
| High | Confirmed | `native/host/src/main.rs:139` | `run` calls `copy::mark(&a.home)` on every start, also when `copy::on()`. `copy::looks_real` (copy.rs:150) returns None as soon as `hub.owner` exists, and `copy::gate` skips `looks_real` entirely on a copy, so the marker is written by the guarded run itself. A whole-folder copy of a Rust-era home carries the marker too. (Also reported by unit 03.) | `scripts/switch.sh` copies the live log into a home and starts it with `BACKPLANE_COPY=1`. After that one run the home holds `hub.owner`, and a later start without the switch is not refused: the hub runs as a real hub on a copied log (interrupted turns resume with real agents in the person's project folders, routines fire, the tailnet listener starts). This is the mistake gate 1 of docs/COPY-GUARD.md exists to stop. | Mark only when not a copy: `if !copy::on() { copy::mark(&a.home) }`. Better, bind the marker to the home (store its canonical path and a random id) so a copied folder does not inherit it. | 02 |
| High | Confirmed | `native/host/src/net/farview.rs:18` | `viewer.pull` drains up to `PULL_BYTES` (6 MiB, and one frame of any size) from the sub's outbox and returns it base64 in one reply, but every direct far call goes through `botnet::args` (botnet.rs:59) with `--max-filesize 1048576`; the hub always sends Content-Length (wire/mod.rs:62), so curl refuses any reply over 1 MiB and `rpc::post` gets an empty body. Same cap for `files.get` (`GET_MAX` 24 MiB, farfiles.rs:16) and a large `git.diff` | Plot or model frames totalling more than about 780 KB raw are removed from the owner's outbox and never reach the window (`pull_loop` parses the empty body as no frames and goes on): the drawing of a real board on another machine stays blank or stale. A file over about 780 KB opened from another machine shows "That machine did not answer." Only links over the reverse tunnel escape the cap | Give `to_peer` a per-call size and time limit, or better an in-process HTTP client; make `pull` stop before the transport limit and send an oversized frame in parts (or keep frames until the next pull acknowledges them); read files in ranges. Until then lower `PULL_BYTES` and `GET_MAX` to what the transport carries | 05 |
| High | Confirmed | `native/host/src/net/outbox.rs:87` | `run` adds a batch only to links that are `own_link` at that moment, but `Out::next` (line 42) still sends `since = acked` while `clean` is true. A link that was not own for a while (asleep, restarting, finder missed its /hello, sync toggled off and on) never had the batches of that period queued, and its `clean`/`acked` are untouched | First push after the link is own again says "follows `acked`" (old) with only the newest items; the mirror's `far::take` sees `since <= head`, takes them and jumps its head to `to`. Every shared line committed while the link was not own is skipped for good: the 5-minute pull asks from the new head. Messages and turn states are silently missing from the far view of a thread until an epoch change | In `run`, for every link the `Outbox` knows that is not in `links`, set `clean = false` (the next push then says `since = from`, the mirror finds the gap and pulls). Or have the feed send every commit's range and let `Out::add` clear `clean` when `from` does not continue the last range. Add a test of `run` with a link that leaves and returns | 05 |
| High | Confirmed | `native/host/src/net/botnet.rs:17` | `url_ok` accepts `http://` for any host, and the pairing by address sends the long-term link secret in clear: `Peering::poll` (pairflow.rs:123) answers `{"state":"approved","invite":"bp1:<addr>:<id>:<secret>"}` to `POST <base>/bots/pair/poll`, and `normalize` (pairflow.rs:44) defaults a typed address to `http://` unless it ends in `.ts.net`. The six-digit check code is made by the asking machine and sent in clear in `/bots/pair/request`, so it authenticates nothing against a party that relays or reads the request. `BACKPLANE_PEERS` and `tailnet::hello` are http only, so the documented path for machines that are not on the tailnet is plain HTTP. | On a local network or any path that is not a tailnet or HTTPS, a passive observer of the pairing reads the secret and can then sign requests as that link (`X-Backplane-Peer: <id>`). For an approved link that is owner trust: start turns, launch threads, read shared thread history, take part in account sync. All later far traffic on such a link is also readable (bodies are signed, not encrypted). | Refuse pairing and far traffic over `http://` unless the host is loopback, a 100.64.0.0/10 address or a `*.ts.net` name; or do a key exchange (X25519 public keys in `/bots/pair/request` and the answer, the secret derived, the check code derived from both keys) so the secret never travels and the check code binds the two ends. | 06 |
| High | Confirmed | `native/host/src/net/peerrpc.rs:49` | Unlinking never clears `peer.own.<id>`. A synced thread with a clean worktree loses its worktree and branch. ## Improvements

Deduplicated from the 556 proposals in the unit files (each unit file has its own table with the rest). Quick wins first: high value and small effort. Effort: S is hours to a day, M is days, L is weeks.

### Quick wins

| Priority | Area | Proposal | Why | Effort | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Copy guard | Write the home marker only when the copy switch is off, and tie the marker to the home (its path, or a hash of the first log line) | One guarded run disarms the guard for that home, and a home copied as a whole folder passes at once (`native/host/src/main.rs:139`, `copy.rs:150`) | S | A home the person moves by hand needs one explicit confirmation |
| P1 | Voice | Refuse always-on listening unless the engine is local, in the hub and in the helper | Room audio stops going to OpenAI against the documented promise (`voice/session.rs:100`, `native/voice/src/live.rs:239`) | S | Always-on is unavailable until the local model is downloaded |
| P1 | Design-file readers | Cap what a file can ask for: vertex and repeat counts and expression depth in the Gerber reader, decoded size of embedded files, node count in VRML; check "regular file under a size limit" before every read of a sheet, board or model | Removes six ways for a downloaded project to take the hub down (`kicad/gerber/rs274x.rs`, `kicad/pcb3d/models.rs:160`, `:489`, `kicad/plotread.rs:119`, `kicad/watch.rs:545`) | S | A very large real file needs the limit raised; take the limits from the largest fixtures |
| P1 | File links | `file.open` refuses executables, app bundles and launcher types, and paths outside the thread's project unless the person confirms | Closes a one-click run of a file an agent wrote (`files/misc.rs:67`, `present/filelink.rs:84`) | S | Opening an unusual file type from a link takes one more step |
| P1 | Settings fields | Send the row's typed text with the Save press, or add the five missing names to the whitelist | Voice and Ring text boxes work on the desktop, and Save stops overwriting the phrase list with an empty one (`present/settings.rs:885`) | S | None |
| P1 | Machine links | Clear `peer.own.<id>` on every unlink path; refuse `http://` for pairing and far traffic unless the host is loopback or a tailnet address | An unlinked machine no longer comes back owner-trusted; the vault, hook secrets and account tokens stop travelling in clear (`net/peerrpc.rs:49`, `net/botnet.rs:17`) | S | Pairing over a plain LAN needs the tailnet or https |
| P1 | Push | Tie each push token to its paired device and drop it on revoke | A revoked phone stops receiving alerts (`push/mod.rs:384`) | S | `DeviceRegistered` gains a device id field; old events stay valid |
| P1 | Desktop client | Clear `older_on` when a thread is selected; do not restart the `down` clock on a timed-out connect; strip `&routes=` and accept `https://` in the first-run chooser | Three High client defects with one-line causes (`native/gpui/src/hub.rs:225`, `:169`, `launch.rs:147`) | S | None |
| P1 | Markdown | Decode `%` escapes on character boundaries and cap HTML nesting | A README can no longer panic or overflow the stack inside the policy state lock (`md/doc.rs:305`, `:318`) | S | None |
| P1 | Agents | Reset the command ledger when a process is stopped or the provider changes | A turn is no longer ended as "completed" 120 s after a failover while the agent still works (`agents/mod.rs:1322`) | S | None |
| P1 | Always-on | Clear the saved Boat id only when the provider answers 404; reuse the idempotency key | A billing sandbox is not orphaned by a timeout or a 5xx (`net/alwayson/make.rs:66`) | S | None |
| P1 | Downloads | Serve `/browser-download` whole, or honour Range on both sides | Downloads over 32 MiB work (`term/browser.rs:55`) | S | None |
| P1 | Mail runs | Make the unattended-run gate default-deny by tool class (read, write, shell, send, persist), and read every tool's real argument names | A mail-started run cannot write files, fetch addresses or type into a terminal unasked (`bots/mail/charter.rs:10-16`) | S to M | Some runs stop to ask until their charter says what they may do |
| P1 | CI and release | Add jobs for `native/voice` tests and the order server's pytest; add `cargo audit` and a small clippy deny list; pin third-party actions by commit; put the release key and keystore in a protected environment; build tags only from commits on main with green CI | Code that no job runs gets tested; the path from any pushed branch to a signed release closes (`.github/workflows/release.yml:7`, `:59`, `:424`) | S each | Slower CI; action updates become a manual step |
| P1 | Docs | Rewrite README "How it works" and "Contributing"; remove or mark the docs that describe the removed tree; correct `docs/store-checkpoints.md` and `docs/voice.md` | Contributors and agents stop being sent to files that do not exist, and the docs stop promising what the code lacks | S | None |
| P2 | Dead paths | Remove `--policy-bin` and `--web` from drivers, the Boat wrappers that call a missing script, the presenter's helper fallback, the web-client routes and the never-called `liveness` tick (or wire it) | Tests that cannot run stop looking like coverage; less code to keep decodable | S | None |
| P2 | KiCad pane | Read only the map state in the 40 ms tick; keep the pixel sample for the e2e report | Removes a full-frame capture 25 times a second (`native/gpui/src/viewers/kicad_pane.rs:679`) | S | None |

### Larger improvements

| Priority | Area | Proposal | Why | Effort | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Agent containment | Give agents and their jobs a lock-only credential instead of a path to the hub's home; deliver the MCP key in a header from a 0600 file, never on the command line or in the URL; record in each message event who wrote it, and let rule checks and approvals read only the person's; make scope widening in `mcp_update` ask | Today an agent can read the pairing token and act as the person, unlock its own enforced rules, and another local user can use a thread's tools (`agents/proc.rs:299`, `agents/mcp.rs:126`, `agents/tools.rs:188`, `mcpx/tools.rs:169`) | M to L | `scripts/bp-lock.sh` and the agents' conveniences that read the home must change; a new event field |
| P1 | Untrusted input out of the hub | Run board, schematic, Gerber, 3D, embedded-file and README work in the existing `bp-plot` child through `proc.rs`, with a memory limit, a deadline and a size check | One boundary instead of a cap in every parser; a crash or runaway costs one read, not the hub | M | A process start per read (cache by content); job objects on Windows |
| P1 | Failure made visible | A hub-wide "writes failing" state: logged, on `/healthz`, shown to clients, checked before a turn or a writing tool starts. Journal the multi-step jobs: home move, `kicad_edit`, merge clean-up, the owed restart after a failover, plan asks | A full disk currently drops turn output silently (97 call sites ignore the commit result), and a restart in the middle of a job leaves state nobody reconciles | M | More events; care that old logs still fold |
| P1 | Editors and the hub's lifecycle | A bounded, ordered stop for embedded KiCad (ask, wait, then kill); restart and update gates that name editors with unsaved work as they name running turns; one table of live sessions so a project with a live editor is never served by a headless KiCad | Unsaved design work is lost on every hub stop, and two KiCad processes can write the same files (`kicad/embed.rs:871`, `:794`, `kicad/ipc.rs:564`) | M | Updates and restarts wait for the person |
| P1 | Hardware threads' folder | One function "the folder a thread's pages show", used by the presenter, `files.*`, `/files/*`, `review.*`, `mech.*` and exports | Review, BOM, Checks and Files of a hardware thread read the code worktree, which no longer holds the design (`files/rv.rs:375`, `files/mod.rs:310`) | M | None |
| P1 | Machine-link protocol | Signature version 2 over method, path, both ids and body, a key per direction, one replay cache for every signed route; a key exchange at pairing; an in-process HTTP client with per-call limits instead of a `curl` process per request | Fixes cross-route replay and reflection, the 1 MiB reply cap that breaks far viewers and files, and the per-request process cost | M to L | A protocol version; both hubs must update |
| P1 | Lock rules | Present from an immutable state snapshot instead of inside `with_state`; a tested rule that no network, process or cross-lane call runs under the bots world lock; fix the two lock-order inversions | A slow file walk or a sleeping peer currently stalls every thread; two inversions can hang a lane until restart (`present/screen.rs:38`, `bots/tools.rs:130`, `data/mod.rs:120`, `bots/swarm.rs:141`) | M | Memory for snapshots |
| P1 | Tests of failure paths | Stand-in agents that fail (exit at start, sign-in and rate-limit errors, partial lines, stalls); a property test that a replayed log gives the live state; hostile-input corpora for every parser on the hub and both phones; update tests for a tampered archive or manifest | The stand-ins cannot produce any error today, so the failure paths the High findings sit on are untested | M | A slower suite |
| P1 | Order server | Check the content type and stream uploads to disk with limits; keep the analysis workers inside the unit's memory cap; ship a DNS name instead of a bare IP and sign quote answers and the pay redirect | Two unauthenticated requests can restart the service; the default server's identity rests on who holds an IP address | M | A domain and a signing step |
| P2 | Listeners | One helper for the main, tailnet and LAN listeners: a deadline for the request head, connection caps, RPCs run off the socket loop | 128 silent sockets lock phones out; a slow request blocks a client's whole connection (`wire/mod.rs:228`, `:326`) | M | None |
| P2 | Device rights | `Caller` names the device; phones get a narrower default (no terminals, no `file.open`, no sign-in shell on another machine) that the person can raise | A lost phone is currently a shell on the machine | M | A settings surface for rights |
| P2 | Parity by data | Move thread order and shelves for phones into the hub; add `data/1` requests for the bot tabs, plan and fab actions | The phones re-implement ordering with different rules and lack whole features | L | Protocol growth on three clients |
| P2 | Observability | A leveled log kept in a rotated file; keep helper programs' stderr; feed the diagnostics recorder | Problems on an always-on hub cannot be diagnosed after the fact; the browser and voice helpers' errors are discarded | M | Disk use; a review of what is logged, for secrets |
| P2 | Code health | Add `rustfmt.toml` and a clippy configuration and run both in CI; split the largest files (`kicad/embed.rs` 235 KB, `settings/accounts_io.rs` 183 KB, `present/screen.rs` 147 KB, `agents/mod.rs` 142 KB) | Lines of up to 5,684 characters defeat review tools and diff views; 454 style notes were recorded across the units | M | A large one-time diff |

## Missing features

From `95-missing-features.md`, ordered Promised, Partial, Inferred, then by value. Categories:

- **Promised**: documented or advertised, but absent or not working.
- **Partial**: started in code but incomplete (stubs, ignored options, one platform or one provider only).
- **Inferred**: not promised, but a user of this kind of product would reasonably expect it. Each has a one-sentence reason tied to how the product is used.

Effort: S is hours to a day, M is days, L is weeks. Paths are relative to the repository root; `H/` means `native/host/src/`, `G/` means `native/gpui/src/`, `A/` means `mobile/android/app/src/main/java/dev/backplane/mobile/`, `I/` means `mobile/ios/Backplane/`.

### Promised

| Feature | Category | Evidence | Value to users | Effort | Dependencies or prerequisites |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Always-on voice keeps audio on the machine | Promised | docs/voice.md:200 ("Audio stays on this machine; nothing is kept or sent before a message starts"); H/voice/session.rs:100-108 starts always-on with whatever engine is set; native/voice/src/live.rs:239 uploads every sound above the energy threshold when the engine is OpenAI (lead; units 39, 40) | High: a privacy promise that is broken in a default-reachable setup (`auto` engine, no local model) | S | Refuse always-on unless the engine is local, in the hub and in the helper |

## Missing tests

The first two test cases each reviewer of a tier 1 or tier 2 hub unit put at the top of its list (723 cases are listed across the unit files). Start with the ones that pin the High findings: the copy-guard marker on a copy run; a failed commit; an agent sending itself the unlock word; hostile Gerber, VRML and embedded files; a hub stop with unsaved editor work; a home move interrupted between retire and adopt; replay equality of the fold.

- (unit 01) A write that fails inside `Hub::writer` (a store whose append errors): every caller of the batch gets `Err`, the model and observers saw nothing, `commit_failed` got the base, the next commit gets the same first line number and succeeds. The test named `a_transform_adds_events_to_the_same_batch_and_is_told_of_a_failed_write` never fails a write.
- (unit 01) `append_many` when the write fails and the cut fails too: the next append's lines read back whole, and `save_index` then `Store::open` give the same lines as a scan. The same on Windows, where the cut is expected to fail.
- (unit 02) A start with `BACKPLANE_COPY=1` on an unmarked, real-looking home leaves no `hub.owner`, and the same home is refused afterwards without the switch.
- (unit 02) A second hub started on a served home changes nothing under `<home>/guard/` and exits with the "already serves" code.
- (unit 03) A copy run (`BACKPLANE_COPY=1`) leaves no `hub.owner`, and a start of that home without the switch is refused.
- (unit 03) A home copied with its marker (file tree copy) is refused without the switch.
- (unit 04) `start_full` with an invalid registry and with a registry of another token: what is installed, whether a listed device can be revoked, whether the tailnet listener starts.
- (unit 04) The stamp policy end to end: `Auth::allowed` for a live device when the home cannot be written, and the status a client sees (today 401).
- (unit 05) `outbox::run`: a link that stops being own for some commits and returns must lead to a gap (or a full pull) on the mirror, not to a push that follows the old `acked`. Nothing tests `run` today.
- (unit 05) A `viewer.pull` reply and a `files.get` reply above 1 MiB over a real signed call between two test hubs (the frames must arrive or stay queued).
- (unit 06) Unlink, then `join` with an invite that reuses the old link id: the new link must not be `verified` (covers `bots.unpeer`, `machine-remove` and the Devices revoke).
- (unit 06) `signed_in`: a signature made for one route refused on another; a request of this hub's own refused when sent back to it; a signature with a timestamp 300 s ahead refused 301 s after it was first accepted.
- (unit 07) `move_to` end to end with two in-process hubs: the receiver stops answering after `home.finish` (the undo's `home.retire` fails), and the sender restarts between the retire commit and `home.at`; assert exactly one live home afterwards and a true row.
- (unit 07) The late pass of the carry: a project thread created before `n0` posts during the copy; assert none of its events and no setting keyed by it is in `extra`.
- (unit 08) Lean against full: one log (with `TodosChanged` by tools, agent and person, `ProviderSet` back and forth, token usage) folded both ways, kept keys equal. Fails today for `todos.<t>`.
- (unit 08) Replay equality: batches through `Policy::before_commit`, then a fresh fold of the returned events equals the live copy, `seq` included; the same after `summary_done` and after `finish_boot` with held batches.
- (unit 09) `view_request`: after Diff replies for several threads the reply and the session stay under a byte budget (regression for the 512 KiB frame limits).
- (unit 09) `view_request`: view.open and view.update of an unknown thread and of a deleted thread answer STALE_VIEW.
- (unit 10) A fork of a thread whose base is behind origin/main: the fork's diff against its HEAD is the source's work only.
- (unit 10) `MergeTicker::tick` end to end on a real fold: a thread that fast-forwarded to a moved main with no commit is not merged; a thread that names another thread's worktree does not remove it; `grep -c name` does not delete the branch `name`; a hand-settled, landed thread; a project that is a folder inside a repository.
- (unit 11) An ask opened more than 4000 log lines ago is still in `thread.entries`, in the thread's `state` and `waiting`, in the badge, and its `AskClosed` reaches the subscription.
- (unit 11) A lock-order test: `with_far` and `with_state(merged_of)` run from two threads while a third folds mirror events, with a deadline.
- (unit 12) A non-Claude turn silent for longer than `BACKPLANE_STUCK_S`, on an actor that earlier ran a lifecycle-mode Claude process stopped by `Agents::stop`, is not ended by the beat.
- (unit 12) A Claude start that waits for an account, on an actor whose earlier process was stopped more than the stuck window ago, is not ended and its retry runs.
- (unit 13) `halt_act` has no test: fresh on a Claude thread whose process is alive must start a new session; compact must reach the CLI as a leading slash command with a standing rule set; a second press and a wrong action for the card are refused.
- (unit 13) A `work_run` without a lock that passes its deadline: `work_cancel` stops it; after a hub restart it is watched again and its end is told; its row does not hold `ready` forever.
- (unit 14) An enforced rule stays closed after `thread_send` to the thread itself and from a sibling, and stays open for the person's word after 60 activity rows.
- (unit 14) `kicad_bring_back` naming a thread of another project, and a deleted thread, is refused.
- (unit 15) A Claude turn, a provider switch, then a driver stand-in that is silent for longer than `BACKPLANE_STUCK_S`: the turn must stay running and end on the driver's own `turn.completed`.
- (unit 15) An ACP stand-in that echoes the `mcpServers` it received, with a vault value in a remote server's header: the value must arrive unmasked (or by the wrapper).
- (unit 16) A hub that dies during a `secret_run` with `files`: after the next `open`, nothing is left under `secrets/run`.
- (unit 16) A value streamed in several chunks is absent from `live_snapshot()`, `live_frame()` and the delta frames (today a test asserts the opposite as a limit).
- (unit 17) A `status` whose CLI times out, is missing or prints no JSON, on a connected account with a turn running: the state, identity and catalog must stay, and the retire watch must cut nothing.
- (unit 17) The same failed check on a sync account, and with a locked token store: its email and plan must survive in memory, in `profiles.json` and in `sync_out`.
- (unit 18) `accounts_sync::serve`: a request without `accounts` changes nothing; `accounts.drop` for an account with a running child answers not removed.
- (unit 18) Two hubs: remove a synced account on the receiver while the maker is away, then bring the maker back: the account stays removed.
- (unit 19) A thread whose provider is second in the order (or not in it) fails over to the first and must come back when its own provider resets.
- (unit 19) `settings_inspect` and `settings_propose` through the MCP server on a hub with the default order and no `BACKPLANE_MODELS`.
- (unit 20) A hardware project thread with a stripped code worktree: `review.bom`, `review.checks`, `mech.list`, `files.op`, `/files/get` must act on the main checkout (today they read the worktree).
- (unit 20) A project in `repo/hw` (a subfolder of its repository): `check_run` with a commit and `release_job` must find the board; today the snapshot folder does not exist.
- (unit 21) A shell that exits while a background job holds the pty (`sh -c 'sleep 30 & exit 0'` as the leader): the terminal must reach `exited 0` and free its slot, and `kill` must end what is left.
- (unit 21) `/browser-download` with a file over `PIECE` (32 MiB): the whole file reaches the caller.
- (unit 22) `bot_list`, `plane_read {machine}` and `room_join {machine}` with a link that does not answer (the `Held` double in tests.rs): another request (`bots.say`, `api::page`) must complete meanwhile.
- (unit 22) `bots.delete` of a bot with a repeating routine, a reminder and a webhook: nothing fires afterwards, the hook's files are gone, the call to its hook is refused; and `bots.delete` of the uberbot is refused, also through data/1 `bot.delete`.
- (unit 23) A claim post with files against the real `Agents` inbox while another thread's `lock_release` grants a lock to a waiting thread (the lock order of swarm.rs:141 and agents/locks.rs:90); today the swarm tests use a fake inbox, so the inversion cannot show.
- (unit 23) A multipart form with `client=ring` on a webhook that is not the ring's: it must arrive as webhook data, not as a spoken message.
- (unit 24) Charter: `risky` is true for `Write`, `Edit`, `WebFetch`; for `Bash` with `rm x`, `git clean -fdx`, `git -C . push`, `bash`, a tab after `curl`, `find . -delete`, `scp`; for `terminal_send {id, text}` and `terminal_open {command: "bash"}`; for `thread_send {threadId: "l1~t2"}` and `bot_send {to: "x@m"}` (the real schemas).
- (unit 24) A run's `memory_save` of kind `pref` does not reach `pref_block`; a run's `routine_set` needs a charter line.
- (unit 25) Hub exit with a stand-in editor that reports a modified frame: the editor is asked and given time before it is killed (today `stock_kicad_ends_when_hub_exits_on_sigterm` only checks that it dies).
- (unit 25) An agent's `kicad_ipc` while the session is `starting`, and while it is `unresponsive`: no headless server is started for that project.
- (unit 26) The `route` arm against the stand-in KiCad: tracks and vias created, `failed` reported, `ripup: true` with a stand-in that still lists deleted items until EndCommit, a crossing net, and the answer when a ripped net cannot be routed again.
- (unit 26) Track breaking with two new ends on one existing track (the stale `extra` copy), and with a new end on a new track.
- (unit 27) Actor: an exit event from an earlier process of a project after a new one was launched must not change the new record (retire while starting; evict then ask again).
- (unit 27) Actor: eviction end to end with a stand-in server: Shutdown accepted, Shutdown refused (held, next victim), no victim left (busy), and a second request during the eviction.
- (unit 28) A board path that is a symlink to a device, a FIFO, or a file over a cap is refused before it is read (after the fix), for `read` and for `prewarm`.
- (unit 28) `bring_back` with a worktree that is only behind main: nothing is listed, nothing is copied.
- (unit 29) Rescue's addresses after a start that died before the tailnet listener bound: with only the loopback line in `listeners`, the last known addresses are still bound (the choice as a pure function).
- (unit 29) `plan::next_on` on probation with `End::Refused`, three `End::Machine` and repeated `End::External`: each goes back to the build before.
- (unit 30) `svc::spec` and `svc::headers` through `Mcp::handle`: answered only for a server in the thread's scope, `spec` refuses a remote server, a stale generation is refused, and a URL whose process has exited is refused (none exists: `svc.rs` has no test module and agents/tests.rs has none for `backplane/mcp_*`).
- (unit 30) `tools::update` as a table: each field that changes what runs, turning on, widening and narrowing of providers, projects and bots, emptying `disabled_tools`; which ask and which apply.
- (unit 31) Route: `GET /oauth/google` with no query while a sign-in waits leaves it waiting.
- (unit 31) Hop: a state naming a ts.net host that is not one of the owner's machines is not forwarded; a forwarded request through a loopback peer with `Host: x.ts.net` and `Tailscale-User-Login` is refused; the untrusted loopback door (`trusted: false`, `Host: 127.0.0.1`) is refused.
- (unit 32) `store::load` with a stored profile that gathered yesterday and a bundled baseline that has a changed rule-less value and a new key: the view must show both.
- (unit 32) `review::checks::stale` after 121 days of successful daily `apply` runs on the jlcpcb and eurocircuits baselines: no finding.
- (unit 33) `pay.checkout`, `_apply`, `confirm`, `expire` and `reconcile` against a Stripe double inside `order/tests`: paid, expired, completed but unpaid, a 404 for a stored session, a timeout, a second Buy now that reuses the open session, two calls at once, the cents sent equal to the quote's total, the country and `expires_at` sent.
- (unit 33) `/q/{id}` for an expired quote, a quote with problems, one with `unavailable`, a paid order, a cancelled order; `/q/{id}/done` with a wrong, a missing and a non-ASCII `s`.
- (unit 34) `session`: a `Select` while a history page is in flight; the next `view.open` reply must be drawn and no `view.update` loop follow (needs the handler extracted).
- (unit 34) The `down` clock: three connect attempts that each time out at 5 s must reach `FAILOVER_AFTER`.
- (unit 35) check-author.sh in a temp repo: good commit, wrong author, wrong committer, jlcjak, `noreply@github.com` committer, a range that does not resolve (must fail), `--ident` with `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL` set.
- (unit 35) land.sh against a bare temp remote: happy path; origin moves during the test (retry loop, three tries); rebase conflict; push rejected by a hook; `LAND_TEST=false` leaves no mark; `LAND_TEST=true` then a plain land must still run the gate; local `main` ahead of origin; no local `main`.
- (unit 36) A CI job that builds the hub with `--features e2e-trust` and runs `test/update/feed.sh` (good, panic, mismatch, older, wrongkey, ceiling) and `test/guard/faults.sh`.
- (unit 36) A release-time check that the packaged CEF runtime is the one the client was linked against, and the smoke job on tag builds before publish.
- (unit 37) The entry merge as a pure function: a failed local echo beside the hub's entry whose id is the clientId, then Retry (ids stay unique); a fresh page that already holds the message drops the echo; `replaces`; an older page overlapping the list.
- (unit 37) 401 at the upgrade followed by a successful `hello` leaves the pairing screen; 403 and 5xx retry; a 401 while on the home hub goes back to the chosen hub once.
- (unit 38) `DataClient` against a scripted socket: a thread opened between `hello` and the end of `synchronise` holds one subscription; thread B pushed over thread A, then A closed, leaves B subscribed and A not.
- (unit 38) A 401 at the upgrade followed by a successful connect leaves no re-pair screen; a 503 is retried; a 401 while on the home hub returns to the chosen hub.
- (unit 39) Always-on with a helper stand-in that exits non-zero at once: the session must back off or stop, not respawn every second, and the error must stay visible.
- (unit 39) Always-on with the cloud engine: either refused, or the consent path is exercised (pins the privacy contract).
- (unit 40) `live --always` with the OpenAI engine: refused with a permanent error (or, until then, a test that pins what is sent).
- (unit 40) A realtime socket closed by the server mid-session: `error` "the connection to OpenAI closed", then `stopping`, `end`, exit 1.
- (unit 41) Renderer crash: kill the tab's renderer (`chrome://crash` or CDP `Page.crash`), then `navigate` must work again on a fresh tab.
- (unit 41) Page-initiated download (`<a download>` clicked by script, no person input) must not produce a `download` event delivered to clients, or must be flagged as not from the person.
- (unit 42) With an editor that checks itself (revisions with `+e<n>`), an external source set just now reads `fresh` in `summary`, and a board-only project's source turns `stale` after a board save.
- (unit 42) Two consecutive identical `ET_SELECTION_CHANGED` events commit at most one `SettingSet` (after a fix).
- (unit 43) `bompanel::data` plus annotation for 400 looked-up lines stays under the info bound, and `publish` reports a refusal.
- (unit 43) `bompanel::live_parts` against a stand-in API that answers a sub-sheet's symbols only for that sheet's document (the stand-in style bomedit's tests use).

## Test, lint and audit results

Nothing in the tree was modified to run these; build output and caches were kept out of the tree.

| What | Result |
|---|---|
| Rust tests of the hub, client, voice helper and guard (`cargo test`, about 4,200 test functions) | **Not run.** This machine (macOS 15, x86_64) has no `cargo` or `rustc`. CI runs the hub and client suites on Linux, macOS and Windows with nextest; no CI job runs the voice helper's tests. |
| Rust lints (`clippy`, `rustfmt`) | **Not run**, and the project does not run them either: there is no `rustfmt.toml`, `clippy.toml` or lint step in any workflow. |
| Rust dependency audit (`cargo audit`, `cargo deny`) | **Not run** (not installed; no toolchain). The lockfiles were read instead: see "Dependency state" in `90-cross-cutting.md`. No advisory database was consulted. |
| Order server (`python3 -m pytest order/tests`) | 24 passed, 8 failed. All 8 failures have one cause in this environment: `python-multipart` is not installed ("The `python-multipart` library must be installed to use form parsing"). Not a code failure. Python here is 3.11.4; the README asks for 3.12 or later. No CI job runs these tests. |
| Message catalogs (`scripts/i18n-check.py`, `scripts/i18n-android.py --check`, `scripts/check-android-i18n.py`, `scripts/i18n-ios.py --check`, `scripts/check-ios-i18n.py`) | All pass: 4,325 keys, every one present in Japanese; the generated Android and iOS catalogs are current. |
| iOS unit tests (`scripts/mobile/ios-unit-tests.sh`, the unit step of the CI ios job) | Exit 0. All 19 suites pass (hub-list, thread-groups 17, badge-symbols 43, thread-find 21, voice-edit 25, plot-parts 27, wire-json 25, term-stream 19, vt 105, term-card 6, migration 18, tr 58, viewer-backdrop 15, browser-input 40, google-loop 21, swarm-model 23, section-menu 6, mcp-page 24, outbox 7). |
| iOS app build (`xcodebuild`) | Not run (it writes derived data into the tree). |
| Android unit tests (`./gradlew testDebugUnitTest`) | **Not run.** Java here is 11 and the build needs 17; Gradle would download its distribution and dependencies and write build folders into the tree. |
| Browser tool and the `.mjs` and `.ts` test drivers | **Not run.** `bun` is not installed. |
| Shell lint (`shellcheck`) | Not run (not installed). A pattern scan was done instead: no `pkill` or `killall`; 68 of 153 shell scripts set `-e`. |
| Secrets in the repository | Pattern scan of every tracked file for AWS, GitHub, OpenAI, Anthropic, Slack, Google and Tailscale keys and private-key blocks: the only matches are test strings in mask tests and placeholder fixtures (`sk-fixture-not-real`). `native/host/src/update/release.pub` is the public release key. No secret scanner (`gitleaks`, `trufflehog`) is installed. |
| Coverage | The project does not measure test coverage. |
| End-to-end drivers in `test/` | Not run (they start hubs, need `bun` or the Rust binaries, and several need the maintainers' machines). |

## Not reviewed

The tree is too large to review all of it in depth (about 18 MB of first-party code). 56 units were read in full and 5 were samples; `coverage.md` has the file list, model and status of every unit. `H/` means `native/host/src/`, `G/` means `native/gpui/src/`, `A/` and `I/` mean the Android and iOS source folders.

### Sampled, not read in full

| # | Unit | KB read / total | Files read | Model | Status |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| 57 | client-screens-a | 232 / 1,744 | G/screens/viewer.rs, timeline.rs, composer.rs, term.rs | sonnet-5.5 | sampled (done) |
| 58 | client-screens-b | 238 / 1,744 | G/screens/projects.rs, settings.rs, accounts.rs, pair.rs, hubpick.rs, onboard.rs | sonnet-5.5 | sampled (done) |

### Skipped

| What | KB | Reason |
|---|---:|---|
| Hub in-crate test files as code: H/agents/tests.rs, agents/tests/*, agents/core_e2e.rs, H/bots/tests.rs, bots/tests/* | 600 | Test code. Each unit's reviewer judges the tests of its own unit for gaps instead of line-reviewing them. |
| G/screens: the other 67 files | 1,270 | View code described by the hub ("the UI is dumb"); the ten largest and most-changed files are sampled in units 57 and 58. |
| Phone view code not listed in 59 and 60 | 1,200 | Same reason; connection, pairing and storage code is in units 37 and 38. |
| scripts/desktop-*, ios-*, android-*, mobile/*, lat/*, android-harness/*, i18n-*.py, check-*-i18n.py, misc one-off rigs | 400 | Developer rigs and screenshot drivers that run on the maintainers' machines; a pattern scan for dangerous commands is done in the cross-cutting pass. |
| test/** drivers other than unit 61 | 2,800 | e2e and visual drivers; sampled in unit 61. |
| Everything in the "Excluded from review" table of 00-map.md | | Vendored, generated, lockfiles, media, fixtures, catalogs, licence texts. |

### Limits of what was reviewed

- No Rust code was compiled or run here (no toolchain), so every finding in Rust comes from reading; "Confirmed" means the code path was traced, not that the failure was reproduced.
- The KiCad fork's source is in another repository; findings that depend on its behaviour are marked Possible, and twelve cross-unit questions stay open, most of them for that reason.
- The removed JavaScript hub, which many comments cite as the specification, is not in this checkout, so "matches the old behaviour" could not be checked.
- Vendored code (`native/gpui/vendor`, `native/host/vendor`, `deploy/kistack`), generated catalogs, fixtures and media were excluded (`00-map.md` lists them).
- The hub's in-crate test files were read for coverage, not line by line.

## Style notes

The unit reviewers recorded 454 style-only notes; they are at the end of each unit file and are not findings. The recurring ones:

- Very long lines (up to 5,684 characters) with several statements or a whole function on one line, and no formatter or linter configuration in the tree.
- Comments that cite files of the removed tree, some with holes where a name was cut ("as in ;"), and module headers that describe an earlier state of the file.
- Leftovers of refactors: `let _ = x;` on values computed and discarded, parameters that are ignored, helpers with no caller.
- The same small helper written two or three times with slight differences (loopback checks, layer names, unit readers, the main checkout's path in scripts).
- User-facing English outside the message catalogs in a few places, and refusal texts that say "not ported" for every refusal.
- Test modules nested at the wrong level, and tests whose expected value is derived from the code under test.
